The function returns an array containing every node exactly once, respecting all edge constraints, and never mutates its arguments. Node.js builtins only, no external packages. ESM.\n\nDeterminism: the result is the unique order produced by repeatedly taking, among all nodes whose prerequisites are already emitted and that are not yet emitted, the alphabetically smallest id (plain `<` comparison on the strings). Nodes with no edges participate in this same selection process.\n\nErrors, checked in this order:\n1. Unknown nodes: if any edge endpoint is not found in `nodes` (using exact string equality), throw `new Error(\"unknown node: x\")` where `x` is the first offending endpoint, scanning edges in order and each edge as `[before, after]` (before first, then after). This check runs over all edges before any ordering work and even when the graph is also cyclic. Duplicate entries in `nodes` are not an error; treat the node list as a set. A duplicate edge (the same `[before, after]` pair appearing more than once) imposes the same single constraint as one copy of that edge and is not an error.\n2. Cycles: if the graph has a cycle, throw `new Error(\"cycle detected: a -> b -> a\")`. The message is `\"cycle detected: \"` followed by a cycle path: an actual node id, then `\" -> \"` repeated, ending with that same starting id again, tracing real edges of the graph through distinct nodes (e.g. `b -> c -> b`). Any cycle and any starting node inside it are acceptable — the message must name real ids joined by `\" -> \"`, start and end with the same id, and every step must follow an edge. A self-edge such as `[\"a\", \"a\"]` is a cycle; its message is exactly `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n\nExamples:\n- `order([\"b\",\"a\",\"c\"], [[\"a\",\"b\"]])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`: \"a\" precedes \"b\", and among the free nodes \"a\" and \"c\" the alphabetically smallest is emitted first, then \"b\" and \"c\" compete.\n- `order([\"c\",\"b\",\"a\"], [])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`.\n- `order([\"a\"], [[\"a\",\"a\"]])` throws `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n- With `nodes [\"a\",\"b\",\"c\",\"d\"]` and edges `[[\"d\",\"b\"],[\"c\",\"d\"]]`, the result is `[\"a\",\"c\",\"d\",\"b\"]`.\n\n\nNote: work carefully and double-check edge cases before finishing."}],"timestamp":1794047526155}}

The function returns an array containing every node exactly once, respecting all edge constraints, and never mutates its arguments. Node.js builtins only, no external packages. ESM.\n\nDeterminism: the result is the unique order produced by repeatedly taking, among all nodes whose prerequisites are already emitted and that are not yet emitted, the alphabetically smallest id (plain `<` comparison on the strings). Nodes with no edges participate in this same selection process.\n\nErrors, checked in this order:\n1. Unknown nodes: if any edge endpoint is not found in `nodes` (using exact string equality), throw `new Error(\"unknown node: x\")` where `x` is the first offending endpoint, scanning edges in order and each edge as `[before, after]` (before first, then after). This check runs over all edges before any ordering work and even when the graph is also cyclic. Duplicate entries in `nodes` are not an error; treat the node list as a set. A duplicate edge (the same `[before, after]` pair appearing more than once) imposes the same single constraint as one copy of that edge and is not an error.\n2. Cycles: if the graph has a cycle, throw `new Error(\"cycle detected: a -> b -> a\")`. The message is `\"cycle detected: \"` followed by a cycle path: an actual node id, then `\" -> \"` repeated, ending with that same starting id again, tracing real edges of the graph through distinct nodes (e.g. `b -> c -> b`). Any cycle and any starting node inside it are acceptable — the message must name real ids joined by `\" -> \"`, start and end with the same id, and every step must follow an edge. A self-edge such as `[\"a\", \"a\"]` is a cycle; its message is exactly `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n\nExamples:\n- `order([\"b\",\"a\",\"c\"], [[\"a\",\"b\"]])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`: \"a\" precedes \"b\", and among the free nodes \"a\" and \"c\" the alphabetically smallest is emitted first, then \"b\" and \"c\" compete.\n- `order([\"c\",\"b\",\"a\"], [])` returns function order(nodes, edges) {\n  // Treat node list as a set.\n  const nodeSet = new Set(nodes);\n\n  // Error 1: unknown node endpoints, scanning edges in order.\n  for (const edge of edges) {\n    const before = edge[0];\n    const after = edge[1];\n    if (!nodeSet.has(before)) {\n      throw new Error(\"unknown node: \" + before);\n    }\n    if (!nodeSet.has(after)) {\n      throw new Error(\"unknown node: \" + after);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Build adjacency and in-degree from unique edges.\n  const adj = new Map();\n  const indeg = new Map();\n  for (const n of nodeSet) {\n    adj.set(n, []);\n    indeg.set(n, 0);\n  }\n  const seen = new Set();\n  for (const edge of edges) {\n    if (seen.has(edge[0] + \"\\u0000\" + edge[1])) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    seen.add(edge[0] + \"\\u0000\" + edge[1]);\n    adj.get(edge[0]).push(edge[1]);\n    indeg.set(edge[1], indeg.get(edge[1]) + 1);\n  }\n\n  // Initial set of nodes with no prerequisites.\n  const available = [];\n  for (const n of nodeSet) {\n    if (indeg.get(n) === 0) {\n      available.push(n);\n    }\n  }\n  // Keep available sorted.\n  available.sort();\n\n  const result = [];\n  let emitted = 0;\n\n  while (available.length > 0) {\n    // Take the alphabetically smallest available id.\n    const cur = available.shift();\n    result.push(cur);\n    emitted++;\n    for (const nxt of adj.get(cur)) {\n      const d = indeg.get(nxt) - 1;\n      indeg.set(nxt, d);\n      if (d === 0) {\n        // Insert into sorted position.\n        let lo = 0;\n        let hi = available.length;\n        while (lo < hi) available.splice(lo, 0, nxt);\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Error 2: cycle detection.\n  if (emitted !== nodeSet.size) {\n    // Compute a cycle message using DFS from an un-emitted node.\n    const cycle = findCycle(nodes, edges);\n    throw new Error(\"cycle detected: \" + cycle);\n  }\n\n  return result;\n}\n\nfunction findCycle(nodes, edges) {\n  const nodeSet = new Set(nodes);\n  const adj = new Map();\n  for (const n of nodeSet) {\n    adj.set(n, []);\n  }\n  const seen = new Set();\n  for (const edge of edges) {\n    const key = edge[0] + \"\\u0000\" + edge[1];\n    if (seen.has(key)) nodeSet) {\n    color.set(n, WHITE);\n  }\n\n  const stack = [];\n  const inPath = new Set();\n\n  function dfs(u) {\n    color.set(u, GRAY);\n    stack.push(u);\n    inPath.add(u);\n    for (const v of adj.get(u)) {\n      if (color.get(v) === WHITE) {\n        const res = dfs(v);\n        if (res) return res;\n      } else if (color.get(v) === GRAY) {\n        // Found cycle, form path from v to stack.pop();\n    inPath.delete(u);\n    color.set(u, BLACK);\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  for (const n of nodeSet) {\n    if (color.get(n) === WHITE) {\n      const res = dfs(n);\n      if (res)
